Appendix J: LFCS Practice Exam — 50 Real Exam-Style Questions
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    J.1  Essential Commands Domain (10 Questions)
         Q1:  Create a directory /var/www/anihpj with permissions 755
              owned by user 'anihpj' and group 'www-data'. Verify.
         Q2:  Find all .py files in /var/www/anihpj/ modified in the
              last 24 hours and count them.
         Q3:  Extract all lines containing "ERROR" from /var/log/syslog
              and save them to /tmp/errors.txt.
         Q4:  Create a compressed tar archive of /var/www/anihpj/
              excluding the __pycache__ directories.
         Q5:  Search /etc/ for all files containing the word "anihpj".
         Q6:  Display the first 5 and last 5 lines of /etc/passwd
              in a single command pipeline.
         Q7:  Count how many unique IP addresses appear in
              /var/log/nginx/access.log.
         Q8:  Replace all occurrences of "localhost" with "anihpj.com"
              in /etc/nginx/sites-available/anihpj.
         Q9:  Find the 10 largest files under /var/log/ and display
              them sorted by size (largest first).
         Q10: Create a file with 1000 lines of "Hello anihpj" using
              only bash built-ins (no external commands).

    J.2  Operation of Running Systems Domain (10 Questions)
         Q11: Check if the nginx service is running and enabled to
              start on boot. If not, start and enable it.
         Q12: A process is consuming 100% CPU. Identify it, determine
              what user owns it, and send SIGTERM.
         Q13: Configure the anihpj systemd service to restart
              automatically after 5 seconds if it crashes.
         Q14: Find all processes owned by user 'anihpj' and display
              their PID, CPU%, MEM%, and command.
         Q15: Set the system to boot into multi-user.target by default
              (not graphical.target).
         Q16: Find which service took the longest to start during the
              last boot.
         Q17: Configure logrotate to rotate /var/log/anihpj/*.log
              daily, keeping 7 days of logs, compressing old ones.
         Q18: View the kernel ring buffer and find any messages
              containing "error" or "fail" (case-insensitive).
         Q19: A process with PID 5678 is stuck. Identify how much
              CPU time it has consumed and what files it has open.
         Q20: Schedule a cron job as the 'anihpj' user to run
              /scripts/cleanup.sh every Sunday at 3 AM.

    J.3  User & Group Management Domain (10 Questions)
         Q21: Create user 'anihpj' with UID 1500, home directory
              /var/www/anihpj, shell /usr/sbin/nologin.
         Q22: Create a group 'developers' with GID 2500. Add users
              'alice', 'bob', and 'anihpj' to this group.
         Q23: Configure sudo to allow user 'anihpj' to restart only
              the nginx and anihpj services without a password.
         Q24: Set password aging for user 'anihpj': max 90 days,
              warn 14 days before expiry, account expires Dec 31 2026.
         Q25: Create a shared directory /projects/anihpj where:
              - Owner is root, group is 'developers'
              - New files automatically inherit the 'developers' group
              - Only owner and group members can read/write
         Q26: Find all users with UID >= 1000 and display their
              username, UID, home directory, and shell.
         Q27: Lock user 'tempuser' account and verify it's locked.
         Q28: Set the default umask for user 'anihpj' to 027.
         Q29: Find all files in /var/www/anihpj/ owned by user
              'www-data' and change them to be owned by 'anihpj'.
         Q30: Create a system user 'postgres-backup' with no home
              directory and no login shell.

    J.4  Networking Domain (10 Questions)
         Q31: Configure a static IP address 192.168.1.100/24 on eth0
              persistently using netplan.
         Q32: Configure the firewall (ufw) to allow only SSH (22),
              HTTP (80), and HTTPS (443). Deny everything else.
         Q33: Test if port 5432 (PostgreSQL) is reachable on
              localhost. If not, identify what's blocking it.
         Q34: Add a persistent route so traffic to 10.0.0.0/8 goes
              through gateway 192.168.1.1.
         Q35: Configure /etc/hosts so 'anihpj.local' resolves to
              127.0.0.1 and verify with ping.
         Q36: Use tcpdump to capture 10 packets on port 80 and
              save them to /tmp/http-capture.pcap.
         Q37: Find which process is listening on port 8000 and
              display its PID, user, and command.
         Q38: Set the system hostname to 'anihpj-prod' persistently.
         Q39: Configure the DNS resolver to use 1.1.1.1 as primary
              and 8.8.8.8 as secondary nameserver.
         Q40: Create an SSH config entry so 'ssh anihpj' connects
              to anihpj.com as user 'deploy' on port 2222.

    J.5  Service Configuration Domain (10 Questions)
         Q41: Configure Nginx to serve /var/www/anihpj/static/ at
              the URL path /static/ and proxy everything else to
              localhost:8000 (Gunicorn).
         Q42: Generate an SSH key pair (ed25519) for the anihpj user
              and configure the server to accept it.
         Q43: Create a self-signed SSL certificate for anihpj.com
              valid for 365 days using OpenSSL.
         Q44: Configure PostgreSQL to listen on localhost only and
              create a database 'anihpj_dev' with owner 'anihpj_user'.
         Q45: Write a systemd service unit that starts a Python HTTP
              server on port 8080 as user 'nobody'.
         Q46: Configure NTP time synchronization using chrony. Set
              the timezone to Africa/Lagos.
         Q47: Restrict SSH to key-based authentication only (disable
              password authentication and root login).
         Q48: Create a new Nginx virtual host for 'api.anihpj.com'
              that reverse proxies to localhost:9000.
         Q49: Backup the PostgreSQL database 'anihpj_db' to a
              compressed custom-format dump file.
         Q50: Deploy the full anihpj stack: create systemd service,
              configure Nginx proxy, test with curl.

    ━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
    📝 DETAILED ANSWERS & EXPLANATIONS
    ━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

    J.1  Essential Commands Domain — ANSWERS
    ─────────────────────────────────────────

    Q1:  mkdir -p /var/www/anihpj
         chmod 755 /var/www/anihpj
         chown anihpj:www-data /var/www/anihpj
         VERIFY: ls -la /var/www/ | grep anihpj
         → drwxr-xr-x 2 anihpj www-data ... anihpj/
         ✅ Domain: Essential Commands — file operations

    Q2:  find /var/www/anihpj -name "*.py" -mtime -1 | wc -l
         VERIFY: The number of .py files modified in the last 24 hours
         ✅ Domain: Essential Commands — find + piping

    Q3:  grep "ERROR" /var/log/syslog > /tmp/errors.txt
         VERIFY: cat /tmp/errors.txt | head
         ✅ Domain: Essential Commands — grep + redirection

    Q4:  tar -czvf /tmp/anihpj-backup.tar.gz \
              --exclude='__pycache__' /var/www/anihpj/
         VERIFY: tar -tvf /tmp/anihpj-backup.tar.gz | head
         ✅ Domain: Essential Commands — archive creation

    Q5:  grep -r "anihpj" /etc/ 2>/dev/null
         (2>/dev/null suppresses permission-denied errors)
         VERIFY: Shows all config files referencing anihpj
         ✅ Domain: Essential Commands — recursive grep

    Q6:  (head -5 /etc/passwd && tail -5 /etc/passwd)
         OR: cat /etc/passwd | { head -5; tail -5; }
         VERIFY: Shows first 5 users then last 5 users
         ✅ Domain: Essential Commands — command grouping

    Q7:  awk '{print $1}' /var/log/nginx/access.log | sort -u | wc -l
         VERIFY: Number of unique IPs that accessed the site
         ✅ Domain: Essential Commands — awk, sort, wc pipeline

    Q8:  sed -i 's/localhost/anihpj.com/g' \
              /etc/nginx/sites-available/anihpj
         VERIFY: grep "anihpj.com" /etc/nginx/sites-available/anihpj
         ✅ Domain: Essential Commands — sed substitution

    Q9:  find /var/log -type f -exec du -h {} \; | sort -rh | head -10
         OR: du -ah /var/log | sort -rh | head -10
         VERIFY: Top 10 largest log files displayed
         ✅ Domain: Essential Commands — find + sort pipeline

    Q10: for i in {1..1000}; do echo "Hello anihpj"; done > /tmp/hello.txt
         wc -l /tmp/hello.txt → 1000
         VERIFY: wc -l /tmp/hello.txt must show 1000
         ✅ Domain: Essential Commands — bash built-ins

    J.2  Operations Deployment Domain — ANSWERS
    ────────────────────────────────────────────

    Q11: systemctl is-active nginx || systemctl start nginx
         systemctl is-enabled nginx || systemctl enable nginx
         VERIFY: systemctl status nginx → active (running); enabled
         ✅ Domain: Operations Deployment — service management

    Q12: top -bn1 | head -20          → find high CPU process
         ps -p PID -o user,pid,%cpu,comm → identify user + PID
         kill -15 PID                 → send SIGTERM (graceful)
         kill -9 PID                  → SIGKILL if SIGTERM fails
         VERIFY: ps -p PID (no longer exists or restarted)
         ✅ Domain: Operations Deployment — process management

    Q13: # In /etc/systemd/system/anihpj.service, add under [Service]:
         Restart=on-failure
         RestartSec=5s
         systemctl daemon-reload && systemctl restart anihpj
         VERIFY: systemctl show anihpj | grep Restart
         ✅ Domain: Operations Deployment — systemd configuration

    Q14: ps -u anihpj -o pid,%cpu,%mem,comm
         VERIFY: Lists all processes with anihpj's UID
         ✅ Domain: Operations Deployment — process inspection

    Q15: systemctl get-default              → check current
         systemctl set-default multi-user.target
         VERIFY: systemctl get-default → multi-user.target
         ✅ Domain: Operations Deployment — boot targets

    Q16: systemd-analyze blame | head -1
         VERIFY: Shows the slowest service and its time
         ✅ Domain: Operations Deployment — boot analysis

    Q17: # Create /etc/logrotate.d/anihpj:
         /var/log/anihpj/*.log {
             daily
             rotate 7
             compress
             missingok
             notifempty
         }
         logrotate -d /etc/logrotate.d/anihpj  → dry-run test
         VERIFY: logrotate -f /etc/logrotate.d/anihpj → force rotate
         ✅ Domain: Operations Deployment — log management

    Q18: dmesg | grep -iE "error|fail"
         VERIFY: Shows kernel messages with errors/failures
         ✅ Domain: Operations Deployment — kernel diagnostics

    Q19: ps -p 5678 -o pid,time,comm  → CPU time consumed
         lsof -p 5678                  → all open files
         ls -la /proc/5678/fd/        → file descriptors
         VERIFY: Shows CPU time and all files held open
         ✅ Domain: Operations Deployment — process forensics

    Q20: sudo crontab -u anihpj -e
         # Add: 0 3 * * 0 /scripts/cleanup.sh
         VERIFY: sudo crontab -u anihpj -l
         ✅ Domain: Operations Deployment — job scheduling

    J.3  Users & Groups Domain — ANSWERS
    ──────────────────────────────────────

    Q21: useradd -u 1500 -d /var/www/anihpj \
              -s /usr/sbin/nologin anihpj
         VERIFY: grep anihpj /etc/passwd
         → anihpj:x:1500:...:/var/www/anihpj:/usr/sbin/nologin
         ✅ Domain: Users & Groups — user creation

    Q22: groupadd -g 2500 developers
         for user in alice bob anihpj; do
             usermod -aG developers $user
         done
         VERIFY: grep developers /etc/group
         → developers:x:2500:alice,bob,anihpj
         ✅ Domain: Users & Groups — group management

    Q23: visudo -f /etc/sudoers.d/anihpj
         # Add: anihpj ALL=(ALL) NOPASSWD: /usr/bin/systemctl restart nginx, /usr/bin/systemctl restart anihpj
         VERIFY: sudo -l -U anihpj
         ✅ Domain: Users & Groups — sudo configuration

    Q24: chage -M 90 -W 14 -E 2026-12-31 anihpj
         VERIFY: chage -l anihpj
         ✅ Domain: Users & Groups — password aging

    Q25: mkdir -p /projects/anihpj
         chown root:developers /projects/anihpj
         chmod 2770 /projects/anihpj  ← 2=SGID, 770=owner+group rwx
         VERIFY: ls -la /projects/ → drwxrws--- root developers
         ✅ Domain: Users & Groups — permissions + SGID

    Q26: awk -F: '$3 >= 1000 {print $1, $3, $6, $7}' /etc/passwd
         VERIFY: Shows all regular (non-system) users
         ✅ Domain: Users & Groups — user enumeration

    Q27: passwd -l tempuser
         VERIFY: grep tempuser /etc/shadow → password field starts with !!
         ✅ Domain: Users & Groups — account locking

    Q28: echo "umask 027" >> /home/anihpj/.bashrc
         OR: echo "umask 027" >> /var/www/anihpj/.profile
         VERIFY: su - anihpj -c 'umask' → 0027
         ✅ Domain: Users & Groups — environment profiles

    Q29: find /var/www/anihpj -user www-data -exec chown anihpj {} \;
         OR: chown -R anihpj /var/www/anihpj/ (if all should change)
         VERIFY: find /var/www/anihpj -user www-data | wc -l → 0
         ✅ Domain: Users & Groups — recursive ownership

    Q30: useradd -r -M -s /usr/sbin/nologin postgres-backup
         (-r = system user, -M = no home directory)
         VERIFY: grep postgres-backup /etc/passwd
         ✅ Domain: Users & Groups — system user creation

    J.4  Networking Domain — ANSWERS
    ──────────────────────────────────

    Q31: # Create/edit /etc/netplan/01-netcfg.yaml:
         network:
           version: 2
           renderer: networkd
           ethernets:
             eth0:
               dhcp4: no
               addresses: [192.168.1.100/24]
               gateway4: 192.168.1.1
               nameservers:
                 addresses: [1.1.1.1, 8.8.8.8]
         netplan apply
         VERIFY: ip addr show eth0 → 192.168.1.100/24
         ✅ Domain: Networking — static IP configuration

    Q32: ufw default deny incoming
         ufw default allow outgoing
         ufw allow 22/tcp
         ufw allow 80/tcp
         ufw allow 443/tcp
         ufw enable
         VERIFY: ufw status verbose → shows all rules
         ✅ Domain: Networking — firewall configuration

    Q33: ss -tulnp | grep 5432        → is it listening?
         systemctl status postgresql    → is the service running?
         ufw status | grep 5432        → firewall blocking?
         nc -zv localhost 5432          → can you connect?
         → Connection refused = service not listening
         → timeout = firewall blocking
         ✅ Domain: Networking — port troubleshooting

    Q34: ip route add 10.0.0.0/8 via 192.168.1.1   ← temporary
         # Persistent (netplan): add under routes:
         routes:
           - to: 10.0.0.0/8
             via: 192.168.1.1
         VERIFY: ip route show | grep 10.0.0.0
         ✅ Domain: Networking — static routing

    Q35: echo "127.0.0.1 anihpj.local" >> /etc/hosts
         ping -c 1 anihpj.local
         VERIFY: ping shows replies from 127.0.0.1
         ✅ Domain: Networking — hostname resolution

    Q36: tcpdump -i eth0 port 80 -c 10 -w /tmp/http-capture.pcap
         VERIFY: tcpdump -r /tmp/http-capture.pcap
         ✅ Domain: Networking — packet capture

    Q37: ss -tulnp | grep :8000
         OR: lsof -i :8000
         VERIFY: Shows PID, user, and command of the process
         ✅ Domain: Networking — socket inspection

    Q38: hostnamectl set-hostname anihpj-prod
         VERIFY: hostnamectl → Static hostname: anihpj-prod
         ✅ Domain: Networking — hostname configuration

    Q39: # Edit /etc/systemd/resolved.conf:
         [Resolve]
         DNS=1.1.1.1 8.8.8.8
         systemctl restart systemd-resolved
         VERIFY: resolvectl status → shows DNS servers
         ✅ Domain: Networking — DNS resolver

    Q40: # Add to ~/.ssh/config:
         Host anihpj
             HostName anihpj.com
             User deploy
             Port 2222
             IdentityFile ~/.ssh/anihpj_ed25519
         VERIFY: ssh anihpj → connects correctly
         ✅ Domain: Networking — SSH client config

    J.5  Essential Commands / Service Config Domain — ANSWERS
    ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

    Q41: # In /etc/nginx/sites-available/anihpj:
         server {
             listen 80;
             location /static/ {
                 alias /var/www/anihpj/static/;
             }
             location / {
                 proxy_pass http://localhost:8000;
             }
         }
         ln -s /etc/nginx/sites-available/anihpj /etc/nginx/sites-enabled/
         nginx -t && systemctl reload nginx
         VERIFY: curl -I http://localhost/static/test.css → 200
         ✅ Domain: Networking — reverse proxy configuration

    Q42: sudo -u anihpj ssh-keygen -t ed25519 \
              -f /var/www/anihpj/.ssh/id_ed25519 -N ""
         cat /var/www/anihpj/.ssh/id_ed25519.pub >> \
              /home/deploy/.ssh/authorized_keys
         chmod 600 /home/deploy/.ssh/authorized_keys
         VERIFY: sudo -u anihpj ssh deploy@localhost 'echo OK'
         ✅ Domain: Networking — SSH key authentication

    Q43: openssl req -x509 -newkey rsa:4096 -nodes \
              -keyout /etc/ssl/private/anihpj.key \
              -out /etc/ssl/certs/anihpj.crt \
              -days 365 -subj "/CN=anihpj.com"
         VERIFY: openssl x509 -in /etc/ssl/certs/anihpj.crt -text -noout
         ✅ Domain: Essential Commands — SSL certificates

    Q44: # Edit /etc/postgresql/15/main/postgresql.conf:
         listen_addresses = 'localhost'
         systemctl restart postgresql
         sudo -u postgres psql -c "CREATE DATABASE anihpj_dev OWNER anihpj_user;"
         VERIFY: ss -tulnp | grep 5432 → 127.0.0.1:5432 (not 0.0.0.0!)
         ✅ Domain: Operations Deployment — service configuration

    Q45: # Create /etc/systemd/system/pyhttp.service:
         [Unit]
         Description=Python HTTP Server
         [Service]
         User=nobody
         ExecStart=/usr/bin/python3 -m http.server 8080
         [Install]
         WantedBy=multi-user.target
         systemctl daemon-reload && systemctl start pyhttp
         VERIFY: curl -I http://localhost:8080 → 200 OK
         ✅ Domain: Operations Deployment — systemd unit creation

    Q46: apt install chrony
         sed -i 's/^pool.*/pool pool.ntp.org iburst/' /etc/chrony/chrony.conf
         systemctl restart chrony
         timedatectl set-timezone Africa/Lagos
         VERIFY: chronyc tracking → shows sync status
         ✅ Domain: Networking — time synchronization

    Q47: # Edit /etc/ssh/sshd_config:
         PasswordAuthentication no
         PermitRootLogin no
         PubkeyAuthentication yes
         sshd -t && systemctl reload sshd
         VERIFY: ssh -o PasswordAuthentication=yes user@localhost → fails
         ✅ Domain: Networking — SSH hardening

    Q48: # Create /etc/nginx/sites-available/api.anihpj:
         server {
             listen 80;
             server_name api.anihpj.com;
             location / {
                 proxy_pass http://localhost:9000;
             }
         }
         ln -s /etc/nginx/sites-available/api.anihpj /etc/nginx/sites-enabled/
         nginx -t && systemctl reload nginx
         VERIFY: curl -H "Host: api.anihpj.com" http://localhost/ → response
         ✅ Domain: Networking — reverse proxy configuration

    Q49: pg_dump -Fc -U anihpj_user anihpj_db \
              > /backups/anihpj_$(date +%F).dump
         VERIFY: pg_restore -l /backups/anihpj_*.dump | head
         ✅ Domain: Operations Deployment — database backup

    Q50: # Create service, start it, configure Nginx, test:
         systemctl start anihpj
         systemctl enable anihpj
         cp /etc/nginx/sites-available/anihpj /etc/nginx/sites-enabled/
         nginx -t && systemctl reload nginx
         curl -I http://localhost/jobs/ → 200 OK
         VERIFY: All services active, web endpoint responding
         ✅ Domain: Operations Deployment — full service deployment


Appendix K: Quick Reference Cards & Cheat Sheets
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    K.1  Permissions Quick Card
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  PERMISSION SET │ OCTAL │ USE CASE                              │
         │ ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── │
         │  -rw-------     │  600  │ Private key, .env file                │
         │  -rw-r--r--     │  644  │ Source code, config files             │
         │  -rwxr-xr-x     │  755  │ Scripts, executables, directories     │
         │  drwx------     │  700  │ Private home directory                │
         │  drwxr-xr-x     │  755  │ Public web directory                  │
         │  drwxrwxr-x     │  775  │ Group-shared directory                │
         │  ----------     │  000  │ Locked file (no access)               │
         │                                                                  │
         │  chmod u+x file      → Add execute for owner                   │
         │  chmod g-w file      → Remove write for group                  │
         │  chmod o= file       → Remove ALL permissions for others       │
         │  chmod -R 755 dir/   → Recursively set (use carefully!)         │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    K.2  systemctl Quick Card
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  TASK                           │ COMMAND                        │
         │ ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── │
         │  Start service NOW              │ systemctl start nginx          │
         │  Stop service NOW               │ systemctl stop nginx           │
         │  Restart service                │ systemctl restart nginx        │
         │  Reload config (no downtime)    │ systemctl reload nginx         │
         │  Check if running               │ systemctl status nginx         │
         │  Enable on boot                 │ systemctl enable nginx         │
         │  Disable on boot                │ systemctl disable nginx        │
         │  Check if enabled on boot       │ systemctl is-enabled nginx     │
         │  Check if running now           │ systemctl is-active nginx      │
         │  List all services              │ systemctl list-units --type=service│
         │  Show service logs              │ journalctl -u nginx            │
         │  Follow service logs            │ journalctl -fu nginx           │
         │  Reload all unit files          │ systemctl daemon-reload        │
         │  Show dependencies              │ systemctl list-dependencies nginx│
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    K.3  find Command Quick Card
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  WHAT YOU WANT                    │ find COMMAND                 │
         │ ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── │
         │  Files by name                    │ find / -name "*.py"         │
         │  Files by owner                   │ find /var -user anihpj      │
         │  Files by group                   │ find /var -group www-data   │
         │  Files by size (>10MB)            │ find / -size +10M           │
         │  Files modified in last 7 days    │ find /var/log -mtime -7     │
         │  Files NOT modified in 30 days    │ find /tmp -mtime +30        │
         │  Directories only                 │ find /etc -type d            │
         │  Files only                        │ find /var -type f            │
         │  Empty files                      │ find /tmp -empty             │
         │  Files with SUID bit set          │ find / -perm -4000          │
         │  Files with 777 permissions       │ find / -perm 777            │
         │  Execute command on results       │ find . -name "*.log" -exec rm {} \;│
         │  Delete matching files            │ find /tmp -name "*.tmp" -delete│
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    K.4  LVM Quick Card
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  TASK                              │ COMMAND                     │
         │ ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── │
         │  Create Physical Volume            │ pvcreate /dev/sdb1          │
         │  List Physical Volumes             │ pvs / pvdisplay             │
         │  Create Volume Group               │ vgcreate vg-data /dev/sdb1 │
         │  Extend Volume Group               │ vgextend vg-data /dev/sdc1 │
         │  List Volume Groups                │ vgs / vgdisplay             │
         │  Create Logical Volume (10GB)      │ lvcreate -L 10G -n lv-app vg-data│
         │  Extend Logical Volume (+5GB)      │ lvextend -L +5G /dev/vg-data/lv-app│
         │  List Logical Volumes              │ lvs / lvdisplay             │
         │  Resize ext4 filesystem            │ resize2fs /dev/vg-data/lv-app│
         │  Resize xfs filesystem (mounted!)  │ xfs_growfs /mountpoint      │
         │  Create LVM snapshot (2GB)         │ lvcreate -L 2G -s -n snap /dev/vg-data/lv-app│
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    K.5  tar Quick Card
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  TASK                          │ COMMAND                         │
         │ ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── │
         │  Create tar archive            │ tar -cvf archive.tar dir/       │
         │  Create .tar.gz (gzip)         │ tar -czvf archive.tar.gz dir/   │
         │  Create .tar.bz2 (bzip2)       │ tar -cjvf archive.tar.bz2 dir/  │
         │  Create .tar.xz (xz, smallest) │ tar -cJvf archive.tar.xz dir/   │
         │  Extract archive               │ tar -xvf archive.tar            │
         │  Extract to specific dir       │ tar -xvf archive.tar -C /opt/   │
         │  List contents (don't extract) │ tar -tvf archive.tar            │
         │  Extract single file           │ tar -xvf archive.tar ./file.txt │
         │  MEMORY AID: c=Create, x=eXtract, t=lisT, v=Verbose, f=File     │
         │              z=gZip, j=bZip2, J=xZ                              │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    K.6  SSH & Key Management Quick Card
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  TASK                          │ COMMAND                         │
         │ ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── │
         │  Generate Ed25519 key pair     │ ssh-keygen -t ed25519 -C "comment"│
         │  Copy key to server            │ ssh-copy-id user@host            │
         │  Test SSH connection           │ ssh -v user@host                 │
         │  SSH with specific key         │ ssh -i ~/.ssh/custom_key user@host│
         │  SSH with custom port          │ ssh -p 2222 user@host            │
         │  Copy file via SCP             │ scp file.txt user@host:/path/    │
         │  Sync directory via rsync      │ rsync -avz dir/ user@host:/path/ │
         │  Fix key permissions           │ chmod 600 ~/.ssh/id_ed25519     │
         │  Fix .ssh directory perms      │ chmod 700 ~/.ssh                │
         │  Test sshd config              │ sshd -t                         │
         │  Reload sshd config            │ systemctl reload sshd            │
         │  Local port forward            │ ssh -L 8080:localhost:80 host   │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    K.7  grep Quick Card
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  TASK                          │ COMMAND                         │
         │ ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── │
         │  Basic search                  │ grep "pattern" file             │
         │  Case-insensitive              │ grep -i "pattern" file         │
         │  Recursive (directory)         │ grep -r "pattern" /path/       │
         │  Show line numbers             │ grep -n "pattern" file         │
         │  Count matches                 │ grep -c "pattern" file         │
         │  Invert match (NOT pattern)    │ grep -v "pattern" file         │
         │  Whole word only               │ grep -w "word" file            │
         │  Show filenames only           │ grep -l "pattern" *.log        │
         │  Context (3 lines before/after)│ grep -B3 -A3 "pattern" file    │
         │  Extended regex (+?{}|)        │ grep -E "ERR|WARN|FATAL"       │
         │  Perl-compatible regex         │ grep -P '\d{3}-\d{4}' file     │
         │  Only matching part            │ grep -o "pattern" file         │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    K.8  LFCS Exam Day One-Pager
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  🎯 LFCS EXAM DAY CHECKLIST                                      │
         │                                                                  │
         │  ☐ Terminal aliases ready (see Exam Day Setup in front matter)   │
         │  ☐ Know: man 5 fstab, man 5 passwd, man 5 crontab               │
         │  ☐ Remember: "persistent" = /etc/fstab, /etc/systemd/system/,   │
         │     /etc/sysctl.conf, /etc/crontab, /etc/profile                 │
         │                                                                  │
         │  ⚡ SPEED COMMANDS (memorize these):                              │
         │    id username                  → quick user info                │
         │    systemctl status service     → quick service check            │
         │    ss -tulnp                    → quick network ports            │
         │    lsblk -f                     → quick disk/FS overview         │
         │    df -hT                       → quick disk usage               │
         │    mount -a                     → test fstab before reboot!      │
         │                                                                  │
         │  ❌ DON'T:                                                       │
         │    - Use GUI tools (they don't exist in the exam VM)             │
         │    - Forget to test persistence (reboot mentally)                │
         │    - Edit /etc files without a backup (cp file file.bak)        │
         │    - Use ifconfig, netstat, route (use ip, ss, ip route)        │
         │    - Panic — skip hard questions, return later                   │
         │    - Forget to verify your work after EACH task                  │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
